# NovaGame M3.5 Administrator Management

## Objective

Complete the framework-owned `Operations/AdminAccess` management surface so an authorized operator
can manage administrator accounts, roles and registered permissions, inspect and revoke
administrator sessions, and inspect administrator-access audit history through the generic Vben
Admin 5 `web-ele` application.

M3.5 is a complete NovaGame server-to-admin vertical slice. It is not a frontend-only task, does
not add game-specific operations, and does not change the completed M3 SwordMaster capability.
It does not authorize extraction, M4 portability work, an admin-shell package, or NovaCore changes.

This file is a repository runbook. It is not a Codex desktop active Goal and must not be registered
as one unless the user separately requests that action.

## Authority And Required Reading

Before starting or resuming a task, read:

1. the root `AGENTS.md`;
2. `server/AGENTS.md` before changing server code;
3. `admin/AGENTS.md` before changing the Vben workspace;
4. `docs/architecture.md`;
5. `docs/workflows/development.md`;
6. the completed `docs/goals/m0.5.md` administrator-access baseline;
7. the completed `docs/goals/m3.md` record only for retained boundary and acceptance context.

The user requested the separately discussed NovaGame administrator-management work on 2026-08-13.
The stable authorization reference is `user-request-2026-08-13-start-admin-management`. This
authorization is limited to the ordered M3.5 tasks below.

## Execution Protocol

- Execute stages and tasks in registered order.
- Keep exactly one stage and one task `in_progress` while the goal is active.
- Mark a task complete only after its deliverable and named verification pass.
- Mark a stage complete only after all of its tasks and cumulative stage checks pass.
- Record a verified task result and create its dedicated commit before activating the successor
  task; after cumulative verification, record and commit each completed stage before activating its
  successor stage.
- Never skip a failed prerequisite or weaken an acceptance criterion.
- Record failed commands and blockers in this goal before continuing.
- Use real MySQL 8.4 for persistence and integration evidence.
- Use the real NovaGame API for browser acceptance; Vben mock responses are not evidence.
- Keep all changes inside the existing `Operations/AdminAccess` domain and generic `admin/` app.
- Do not add SwordMaster routes, permissions, terminology, APIs, or views to the generic admin.
- Complete M3.5 only after M35-08 passes and execution is deactivated.

Allowed goal statuses are `drafting`, `ready`, `in_progress`, `blocked`, and `complete`. Allowed
stage and task statuses are `pending`, `in_progress`, `blocked`, and `complete`.

## Preconditions

- M0.5, M1, M2, and M3 remain complete and inactive.
- No other repository goal is active.
- NovaCore remains clean and is not modified by M3.5.
- The M0.5 authentication, MySQL, GameConfig, and Vben baselines pass.
- .NET 8, Docker with Compose, Node.js 24.14.0, pnpm 10.33.4, and an approved Playwright browser are available.
- Local credentials remain in ignored environment files or process environment variables.
- M4 remains `Unscheduled`.

## Frozen Scope

M3.5 extends the existing framework-owned `Operations/AdminAccess` capability across the existing
six server layers and the generic `admin/apps/web-ele` application. It does not create a physical
module or another authentication system.

### Administrator Accounts

- List and filter administrators with explicit paging.
- Create an administrator with a server-hashed initial password and selected active roles.
- Edit the display name and role memberships.
- Activate and deactivate accounts.
- Reset another administrator's password.
- Prevent self-deactivation, self-role mutation, and operations that would leave no active built-in administrator.
- Revoke active refresh-token families when an account is deactivated or its password is reset.

### Roles And Permissions

- List roles and their effective registered permission assignments.
- Create, rename, activate, and deactivate custom roles.
- Replace a custom role's permission set using names from the runtime permission-definition catalog.
- Keep the built-in `Administrators` role active, immutable, and synchronized with all registered permissions.
- Reject unknown permission names and duplicate role names.

### Sessions

- List administrator refresh-token families without exposing token values or hashes.
- Show bounded operational metadata and revocation state.
- Revoke an exact active family and make repeated revocation safe.
- Keep access-token lifetime and existing refresh rotation semantics unchanged unless the M35-02
  security design proves a required compatible change.

### Audit

- Query administrator-access audit records with paging and bounded filters for time, action,
  success, actor, subject administrator, role, and correlation identifier.
- Audit every successful administrator, role, permission, password, and forced-session mutation in
  the same MySQL transaction as authoritative state.
- Keep secrets, passwords, token values, token hashes, request bodies, and connection strings out
  of audit data and logs.

### API And Admin

- Add explicit DTOs and thin HTTP endpoints under the existing administrator-authenticated API.
- Add stable permission and error codes, server-side authorization, optimistic concurrency, and
  idempotency where retries can create duplicate authoritative state.
- Keep OpenAPI accurate and generate the M3.5 TypeScript contracts through the frozen checked
  `@hey-api/openapi-ts` workflow rather than maintaining an unchecked duplicate model.
- Add compact Vben `web-ele` workspaces for Administrators, Roles and permissions, Sessions, and
  Audit, with route and action visibility driven by server permission codes.
- Provide loading, empty, error, unauthorized, forbidden, validation, conflict, retry, and
  confirmation states at supported desktop and mobile widths.

Detailed aggregate, transaction, endpoint, ETag, idempotency, permission, error, query, and UI
contracts are frozen in M35-02 before implementation begins. M35-02 may clarify these mechanics but
must not broaden this capability list.

## Fixed Boundaries

- NovaGame owns this capability because `Operations/AdminAccess` is part of the verified baseline.
- Framework server source remains under the existing layered `Operations/AdminAccess` folders.
- Generic operations pages remain under `admin/apps/web-ele`; game-owned pages remain in each game.
- Do not create `samples/SwordMaster/AGENTS.md` or change SwordMaster behavior.
- Do not add Battle, Skills, Enhancement, Set Bonuses, Drops, Shop, Mail, Ranking, Activity,
  player search, rewards, economy, or other game-owned operations.
- Do not extract `@novagame/admin-shell`, promote an experimental contract, or create a new package.
- Do not modify NovaCore, its source checkout, or its locked package snapshot.
- Do not replace MySQL evidence with an in-memory provider or real browser evidence with mocks.

This audit describes commit `42d1ffe83770571d8f48cdb58f35bfc591a4bd1c`. It records only
observable source, schema, and test behavior. It is not the M35-02 implementation design.

| Role aggregate | `AdminRole` owns normalized unique name, built-in marker, creation time, and idempotent permission grants. | [`AdminRole.cs`](../../server/src/NovaGame.Domain/Operations/AdminAccess/AdminRole.cs) | It has no rename, active state, revision, permission revocation/replacement, or built-in-role mutation guard. |

The current GameConfig permission fixture mutates disposable MySQL directly and is baseline evidence, not a substitute for M3.5 management APIs. |

### Audit Conclusion

The baseline already owns authentication, authorization evaluation, relational entities, secure
refresh-token rotation, append-only audit writes, and the generic Vben shell. M3.5 therefore extends
the existing vertical slice. It must not introduce parallel administrator entities, a second token
system, a new physical module, another admin application, or replacement authentication routes.

## M35-01 Gap And Boundary Matrix

| ID | Approved outcome | Current gap | Owning boundary | M35-02 design obligation | Explicit rejection |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M35-GAP-01 | Manage administrator accounts. | No list, create, display-name change, role replacement, activation, deactivation, password-reset, revision, or management API exists. | Existing NovaGame `Operations/AdminAccess` Domain, Application, persistence, and API layers. | Freeze aggregate transitions, self-lockout and last-active-built-in protection, password hashing, session revocation, optimistic concurrency, transaction, audit, DTO, and HTTP behavior. | No delete, bulk import, player account, SwordMaster account, or alternate identity system. |
| M35-GAP-02 | Manage custom roles and registered permissions. | Role supports creation and grant only; no active state, rename, replacement, management query, catalog API, or management permissions exist. | Existing `AdminRole` aggregate and runtime permission-definition boundary. | Freeze built-in-role immutability/synchronization, custom-role transitions, membership effects, registered-permission catalog, concurrency, audit, and APIs. | No tenant hierarchy, game-policy ownership, dynamic permission invention, or physical module. |
| M35-GAP-03 | Inspect and revoke sessions. | Secure family state and lock queries exist only for authentication internals; no redacted family summary, operator permission, exact-family command, or API exists. | Existing `RefreshTokenSession` aggregate and AdminAccess Application/API. | Freeze grouping, metadata exposure, exact revocation semantics, repeated-command behavior, account/password revocation interaction, paging, authorization, and audit. | No token plaintext/hash exposure, access-token denylist, device fingerprinting, or second session store. |
| M35-GAP-04 | Query administrator-access audit history. | Audit writes exist, but the repository has no query and there are no filter DTOs, permissions, API, or admin view. | Existing append-only AdminAccess audit model and read-only query path. | Freeze bounded filters, sort, paging, DTO redaction, indexes, authorization, and transport semantics. | No audit mutation/deletion, arbitrary SQL/report builder, payload capture, or secret logging. |
| M35-GAP-05 | Operate through generic Vben `web-ele`. | The app has auth and GameConfig only; management transports, permission constants, routes, views, component tests, and E2E scenarios are absent. | Existing `admin/apps/web-ele` operational client. | Freeze four compact workspaces, route/action permissions, states, stale-response handling, contract generation, responsive layouts, and real-browser scenarios. | No SwordMaster navigation, game-owned pages, second admin app, marketing dashboard, or admin-shell extraction. |
| M35-GAP-06 | Preserve stable baseline behavior. | New management mutations can affect current login, permission, seed, refresh, and GameConfig paths if designed carelessly. | Existing public auth routes, database permission checker, seed contributor, admin auth store, and GameConfig workspace. | Freeze compatible route additions, permission evaluation timing, seed migration, regression tests, OpenAPI drift checks, and exact cumulative acceptance. | No auth rewrite, refresh-cookie change without evidence, GameConfig redesign, NovaCore change, contract promotion, or M4 work. |

### Ownership Decision

All six gaps belong to the already verified NovaGame-owned `Operations/AdminAccess` capability or
its generic operational client. No extraction workflow applies because the work is not promoting a
game-owned mechanism. No new physical module is justified: the entities, tables, permission
checker, seed, authentication API, and admin shell already reside in the existing layer structure.

M35-02 may select exact contract mechanics only within these outcomes. It cannot add game-owned
operations, a second consumer, a shell package, NovaCore work, or portability work. M4 remains
independent and `Unscheduled`. The completed M3 SwordMaster capability and possible
`request-audit-context-consumer-contract` extraction candidate remain unchanged.

## M35-02 Frozen Domain And Security Contract

This section freezes `M35-02-T01`. Later M35-02 contracts must implement these rules without
moving them out of their authoritative owners.

### Aggregate Ownership And Revisions

- `Administrator` remains the aggregate root for immutable user name and normalized user name,
  display name, password hash, active state, created, updated and last-login UTC timestamps,
  role-membership children, and a new `Revision` starting at `1`.
- `AdminRole` remains the aggregate root for name and normalized name, immutable built-in marker,
  active state, created and updated UTC timestamps, permission-grant children, and a new `Revision`
  starting at `1`.
- Administrator creation includes its initial canonical memberships; role creation includes its
  initial canonical permissions. Initial children belong to revision `1`.
- One changed aggregate command increments its revision exactly once regardless of child deltas.
  Login-time recording does not change the management revision; password rehash does.
- Canonical target-state setters are intrinsically idempotent. If requested state already equals
  current state, no timestamp, revision, child, or audit changes, even with an older supplied
  revision. A request that changes state must match current revision or fail `RevisionConflict`.
- Self-protection and built-in-role rules are checked before an apparent no-op and cannot be
  bypassed by submitting existing state.

### Administrator Transitions

- User names never change after creation. Display names are trimmed, contain `1..128`
  non-control characters, and may be changed through a target-state transition.
- Role replacement receives unique, non-empty role IDs; an empty set is valid. Every requested
  role must exist and be active. Unchanged memberships retain evidence; removed memberships are
  deleted; new memberships receive server IDs, authenticated actor, and server UTC time.
- An administrator can never replace their own roles, deactivate themselves, or reset their own
  password. Deactivation or role replacement may never leave fewer than one active administrator
  assigned to the built-in `Administrators` role.
- Activation and deactivation are target-state setters. Deactivation revokes every active refresh
  family for the target in the same transaction. Repeated deactivation remains an aggregate no-op
  but cleans any residual active family without duplicate audit.
- Password reset receives plaintext only at the Application boundary, hashes before mutation,
  always changes the credential and revision, and revokes every target family in the same
  transaction. Plaintext is never persisted or logged. Inactive accounts may be reset;
  reactivation never restores sessions.

### Roles And Registered Permissions

- The sole built-in role is exactly `Administrators`, is always active, and never changes name or
  built-in marker. Operator rename, state, or permission replacement attempts fail
  `BuiltInRoleImmutable`, including apparent no-ops.
- A system-only seed transition synchronizes that role to the exact ordinal runtime permission
  catalog, preserves unchanged grants, delta-adds/removes, advances once only when changed, and is
  a no-op on a second run. Missing, duplicate, or malformed built-in state with existing
  administrators is `InvariantViolation`; it is not silently recreated.
- Custom role names are FormKC-normalized and trimmed, contain `1..64` non-control characters,
  and use invariant-uppercase uniqueness. Custom roles may be renamed, activated, or deactivated.
- Deactivation retains memberships and grants. Authorization ignores inactive roles, so their
  permissions stop immediately and resume after activation.
- Custom-role permission replacement accepts a unique, case-sensitive subset of registered names;
  empty is valid. Unchanged grants retain evidence; add/remove deltas advance revision once.
  Unknown names fail `UnknownPermission`. Catalog-removed names are ineffective and are pruned by
  later replacement or built-in synchronization.

### Session Families

- `RefreshTokenSession` continues to own one hashed-token row; no second store or family aggregate
  is added. A stateless domain transition operates over all transactionally locked rows with one
  `FamilyId` and requires one administrator owner.
- Family revocation changes all unrevoked rows, including expired rows, without overwriting prior
  evidence. Add `PasswordReset` and `ForcedByAdministrator`; deactivation uses
  `AdministratorInactive`.
- Unknown family fails `SessionFamilyNotFound`. A fully revoked family succeeds as a no-op and
  adds no audit. Operators may revoke their own family as a security action.
- Revocation prevents refresh but does not deny an already-issued access token during its existing
  15-minute lifetime. Database active-account and permission checks continue applying on protected
  requests.

### Policy, Audit, And Failures

- `AdminAccessManagementPolicy` owns actor/target, active built-in count, active requested-role,
  built-in identity, and registered-permission checks over transactionally locked facts.
  Application supplies authenticated actor, clock, generated IDs, catalog, and locks; clients
  never supply identity, time, counts, or authoritative permission state.
- Audit `AdministratorId` means subject and `ActorAdministratorId` means actor. Add nullable
  `SessionFamilyId` and `TargetRevision`. Add actions `AdministratorDisplayNameChanged`,
  `AdministratorActivated`, `AdministratorDeactivated`, `AdministratorPasswordReset`,
  `RoleRemoved`, `RoleRenamed`, `RoleActivated`, `RoleDeactivated`, and `SessionFamilyRevoked`.
- Emit one audit per changed aggregate transition, one membership/permission audit per delta, and
  one family audit per actually changed family. State and audit commit atomically. No-op and replay
  add no audit. Failed commands use structured logs and rolled-back evidence, not success audit.
- Audit/logs never contain passwords, token values or hashes, request bodies, signing keys,
  connection strings, or production credentials.
- Management uses `AdminAccessFailureKind`: `BadRequest`, `Unauthorized`, `Forbidden`, `NotFound`,
  `Conflict`, `UnprocessableEntity`, `InternalError`. Stable new codes are `InvalidRequest`,
  `AdministratorNotFound`, `RoleNotFound`, `SessionFamilyNotFound`, `UserNameAlreadyExists`,
  `RoleNameAlreadyExists`, `RevisionConflict`, `IdempotencyConflict`,
  `SelfDeactivationForbidden`, `SelfRoleMutationForbidden`, `SelfPasswordResetForbidden`,
  `LastActiveBuiltInAdministratorRequired`, `BuiltInRoleImmutable`, `RoleInactive`,
  `UnknownPermission`, and `InvariantViolation`. Existing authentication codes remain stable.

## M35-02 Frozen Application And HTTP Contract

This section freezes `M35-02-T02`. The Application layer owns authorization and use-case
coordination. HTTP remains a thin translation boundary. Neither transport nor clients supply the
authenticated actor, server time, generated identifiers, normalized names, registered-permission
catalog, active-built-in count, or authoritative current revision.

### Permission Definitions And Evaluation

The complete M3.5 permission set is:

| Permission | Authorized surface |
| --- | --- |
| `Operations.AdminAccess.Access` | Existing authenticated operations shell, `/api/auth/codes`, `/api/user/info`, and prerequisite for every `/api/admin-access/*` endpoint. |
| `Operations.AdminAccess.Administrators.Read` | List and inspect administrators. |
| `Operations.AdminAccess.Administrators.Manage` | Create administrators and change display name, roles, or active state. |
| `Operations.AdminAccess.Administrators.ResetPassword` | Reset another administrator's password. |
| `Operations.AdminAccess.Roles.Read` | List and inspect roles and the registered permission catalog. |
| `Operations.AdminAccess.Roles.Manage` | Create or mutate custom roles and permission assignments. |
| `Operations.AdminAccess.Sessions.Read` | List redacted refresh-token families. |
| `Operations.AdminAccess.Sessions.Revoke` | Revoke one exact refresh-token family. |
| `Operations.AdminAccess.AuditLogs.Read` | Query administrator-access audit history. |

`Access` and the endpoint leaf permission are both required and may come from different active
roles. Leaf permissions never imply `Access`, the catalog and UI never auto-add it, and `Access`
alone reveals no management route. Controller policies provide an outer guard; every Application
use case independently resolves the authenticated administrator and checks `Access` followed by
the leaf permission before normalizing target identifiers, looking up an idempotency receipt, or
querying sensitive state. An unauthenticated request returns `401`; an authenticated request
missing either permission returns `403`, regardless of target existence, request revision, or
idempotency history.

After authorization, commands execute in this order: validate and canonicalize transport input;
resolve an exact idempotent replay when applicable; acquire the frozen MySQL locks; resolve target
and related facts; enforce actor/target and built-in policy; apply revision and domain transitions;
append audit and receipt evidence; commit once. Queries authorize before validating filters and
calling repositories. Clients can therefore never use `400`, `404`, `409`, or `422` to probe a
management resource without the required permission.

### Contract Limits, Paging, And Ordering

- Every page is one-based, defaults to `Page = 1` and `PageSize = 20`, and permits a maximum page
  size of `100`. `AdminAccessPageDto<T>` contains `Items`, `TotalCount`, `Page`, and `PageSize`.
- Search and correlation filters are trimmed and limited to `128` characters. No endpoint accepts
  a client-selected sort expression or unbounded query.
- Administrator user names retain the existing `3..64` rule; display names are `1..128`; custom
  role names are `1..64`; managed passwords are `12..256` characters.
- Role-ID collections contain at most `100` unique, non-empty IDs. Permission-name collections
  contain at most `512` unique, non-empty, case-sensitive names of at most `128` characters.
  Duplicate collection values are `InvalidRequest`; the server canonicalizes accepted values by
  ordinal order before comparison or fingerprinting.
- Administrator pages order by normalized user name ascending, then identifier ascending. Role
  pages put the built-in role first, then order by normalized name and identifier ascending.
  Session-family pages order by last activity descending, then family identifier ascending. Audit
  pages order by occurrence descending, then audit identifier descending.
- Audit queries require both `FromUtc` and `ToUtc` as ISO-8601 UTC values, use the half-open range
  `[FromUtc, ToUtc)`, require `FromUtc < ToUtc`, and permit at most `31` days. Query timestamps are
  filters only and never become authoritative mutation time.

### Request And Response DTOs

The management contracts are explicit Application.Contracts types and never expose aggregates or
EF entities:

| DTO | Exact fields |
| --- | --- |
| `AdminRoleReferenceDto` | `Id`, `Name`, `IsBuiltIn`, `IsActive`. |
| `AdministratorDto` | `Id`, immutable `UserName`, `DisplayName`, `IsActive`, ordinal `Roles`, `CreatedAtUtc`, nullable `UpdatedAtUtc`, nullable `LastLoginAtUtc`, positive `Revision`. No password field exists. |
| `AdminRoleSummaryDto` | `Id`, `Name`, `IsBuiltIn`, `IsActive`, effective registered `PermissionCount`, total `AdministratorCount`, `CreatedAtUtc`, nullable `UpdatedAtUtc`, positive `Revision`. |
| `AdminRoleDto` | Summary fields plus ordinal `PermissionNames`; removed catalog names are not returned as effective assignments. |
| `PermissionGroupDto` | `Name`, nullable `DisplayName`, and ordinal `Permissions`. |
| `PermissionDefinitionDto` | `Name`, nullable `DisplayName`, nullable `Description`. |
| `AdminSessionFamilyDto` | `FamilyId`, `AdministratorId`, `UserName`, `DisplayName`, `FirstCreatedAtUtc`, `LastActivityAtUtc`, `ExpiresAtUtc`, nullable `LastIpAddress`, `State`, nullable `RevokedAtUtc`, nullable `RevocationReason`. `State` is server-computed `Active`, `Expired`, or `Revoked`: any unrevoked, unexpired row makes the family active; otherwise a revoked terminal row makes it revoked; otherwise its terminal row is expired. Normal rotation of predecessor rows never makes a family revoked. Metadata comes from the terminal row and latest family activity. No token, token hash, session-row ID, or replacement-chain value exists. |
| `AdminAccessAuditRecordDto` | `Id`, `Action`, `OccurredAtUtc`, `Succeeded`, nullable `SubjectAdministratorId`, nullable `ActorAdministratorId`, nullable `RoleId`, nullable `SessionFamilyId`, nullable `SubjectUserName`, nullable `PermissionName`, nullable `ErrorCode`, nullable `IpAddress`, nullable `UserAgent`, nullable `CorrelationId`, nullable `TargetRevision`. No request body or secret field exists. |

List requests contain these exact filters in addition to `Page` and `PageSize`:

| Request | Filters |
| --- | --- |
| `ListAdministratorsRequest` | nullable user-name-prefix `Search`, nullable `IsActive`, nullable `RoleId`. |
| `ListAdminRolesRequest` | nullable name-prefix `Search`, nullable `IsActive`, nullable `IsBuiltIn`. |
| `ListAdminSessionFamiliesRequest` | nullable `AdministratorId`, nullable `State`. |
| `ListAdminAccessAuditRecordsRequest` | required `FromUtc`, required `ToUtc`, nullable `Action`, nullable `Succeeded`, nullable `ActorAdministratorId`, nullable `SubjectAdministratorId`, nullable `RoleId`, nullable `CorrelationId`. |

Command bodies are `CreateAdministratorRequest(UserName, DisplayName, InitialPassword, RoleIds)`,
`ChangeAdministratorDisplayNameRequest(DisplayName)`,
`ReplaceAdministratorRolesRequest(RoleIds)`, `SetAdministratorActiveStateRequest(IsActive)`,
`ResetAdministratorPasswordRequest(NewPassword)`,
`CreateAdminRoleRequest(Name, PermissionNames)`, `RenameAdminRoleRequest(Name)`,
`SetAdminRoleActiveStateRequest(IsActive)`, and
`ReplaceAdminRolePermissionsRequest(PermissionNames)`. Expected revisions, actor identity,
timestamps, generated IDs, active-role facts, permission definitions, and audit metadata are never
accepted in a body.

### Routes And Use Cases

All management responses use the existing `ApiResponse<T>` envelope and return `200` on success.
Entity create, detail, and mutation responses carry the full redacted entity DTO and its strong
revision ETag. There are no delete or bulk endpoints.

| Method and route | Leaf permission | Request and response |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `GET /api/admin-access/administrators` | `Administrators.Read` | `ListAdministratorsRequest` -> `AdminAccessPageDto<AdministratorDto>`. |
| `POST /api/admin-access/administrators` | `Administrators.Manage` | `CreateAdministratorRequest` plus `Idempotency-Key` -> `AdministratorDto`. |
| `GET /api/admin-access/administrators/{administratorId}` | `Administrators.Read` | `AdministratorDto`. |
| `PUT /api/admin-access/administrators/{administratorId}/display-name` | `Administrators.Manage` | `If-Match` plus display-name request -> `AdministratorDto`. |
| `PUT /api/admin-access/administrators/{administratorId}/roles` | `Administrators.Manage` | `If-Match` plus role-replacement request -> `AdministratorDto`. |
| `PUT /api/admin-access/administrators/{administratorId}/active-state` | `Administrators.Manage` | `If-Match` plus active-state request -> `AdministratorDto`. |
| `POST /api/admin-access/administrators/{administratorId}/password-reset` | `Administrators.ResetPassword` | `If-Match`, `Idempotency-Key`, and password-reset request -> `AdministratorDto`. |
| `GET /api/admin-access/roles` | `Roles.Read` | `ListAdminRolesRequest` -> `AdminAccessPageDto<AdminRoleSummaryDto>`. |
| `POST /api/admin-access/roles` | `Roles.Manage` | `CreateAdminRoleRequest` plus `Idempotency-Key` -> `AdminRoleDto`. |
| `GET /api/admin-access/roles/{roleId}` | `Roles.Read` | `AdminRoleDto`. |
| `PUT /api/admin-access/roles/{roleId}/name` | `Roles.Manage` | `If-Match` plus rename request -> `AdminRoleDto`. |
| `PUT /api/admin-access/roles/{roleId}/active-state` | `Roles.Manage` | `If-Match` plus active-state request -> `AdminRoleDto`. |
| `PUT /api/admin-access/roles/{roleId}/permissions` | `Roles.Manage` | `If-Match` plus permission-replacement request -> `AdminRoleDto`. |
| `GET /api/admin-access/permissions` | `Roles.Read` | No body -> ordinal `IReadOnlyList<PermissionGroupDto>`. |
| `GET /api/admin-access/sessions` | `Sessions.Read` | `ListAdminSessionFamiliesRequest` -> `AdminAccessPageDto<AdminSessionFamilyDto>`. |
| `POST /api/admin-access/sessions/{familyId}/revoke` | `Sessions.Revoke` | No body, ETag, or idempotency key -> current `AdminSessionFamilyDto`. |
| `GET /api/admin-access/audit-records` | `AuditLogs.Read` | `ListAdminAccessAuditRecordsRequest` -> `AdminAccessPageDto<AdminAccessAuditRecordDto>`. |

Role and permission choices always come from the two authorized read endpoints. A caller of a
write endpoint is not implicitly granted either read permission. The admin UI therefore requires
the relevant read permission to render a selector even though direct server mutation requires only
`Access` and its named write permission.

### Revisions And Strong ETags

Administrator and role responses expose a positive `Revision`. Create, detail, and mutation
responses set exactly `ETag: "<revision>"`. Every existing-administrator or existing-role command
requires exactly one `If-Match` value in that same strong, quoted, canonical positive-decimal form;
weak tags, `*`, lists, zero, negative, missing, or non-canonical values fail `InvalidRequest`.
Bodies contain no duplicate expected revision.

After all policy checks, a state-changing command whose expected revision is stale fails
`RevisionConflict`. A canonical target-state request already equal to current state succeeds with
the current DTO and ETag even when its supplied revision is older, adds no audit, and does not
increment revision. Self-protection and built-in-role policy run before no-op recognition. Password
reset always changes state, so a non-replay reset requires the exact current revision. List DTO
revisions may seed an `If-Match`, but the client must handle a concurrent `409` and must never
calculate a successor revision.

### Idempotency Receipts

`POST` administrator creation, role creation, and password reset require exactly one non-empty GUID
`Idempotency-Key` in canonical hyphenated `D` form. The durable unique scope is
`(ActorAdministratorId, CommandKind, IdempotencyKey)`. Each accepted command stores an ordinal
SHA-256 fingerprint of canonical non-secret intent, target resource ID, resulting revision, and
server UTC creation time atomically with state and audit.

The administrator-create fingerprint contains normalized user name, normalized display name, and
sorted role IDs. The role-create fingerprint contains normalized role name and sorted permission
names. The password-reset fingerprint contains target administrator ID and expected revision but
deliberately excludes plaintext, password hash, and any fast password digest. The receipt stores no
secret, request body, response body, token material, IP address, or user agent.

An accepted replay with the same non-secret fingerprint performs no mutation, hashing, revision
increment, session revocation, or audit; it returns `200`, the target's current redacted
representation, and current ETag. Reusing a key with a different non-secret fingerprint fails
`IdempotencyConflict`. For administrator creation and password reset, the first accepted secret
wins: a retry with the same key never applies another supplied password, even if that value
differs, and changing initial-password or reset-password intent requires a new key. Concurrent
matching requests serialize so exactly one mutates and the others replay. Failed commands create
no receipt.
Replay lookup occurs after authorization and canonical validation but before current-revision or
target-state evaluation, allowing a lost successful response to replay after the target advanced.

No other M3.5 command uses a receipt. Canonical aggregate setters and family revocation are already
intrinsically retry-safe and produce no duplicate audit on a repeated target-state request.

### Failure And Compatibility Mapping

| HTTP status | Stable management error codes |
| --- | --- |
| `400` | `NovaGame.AdminAccess.InvalidRequest`; automatic model-binding and data-annotation failures retain `NovaCore.ValidationError`. |
| `401` | Existing `NovaGame.AdminAccess.Unauthorized` with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`. |
| `403` | Existing `NovaGame.AdminAccess.Forbidden`. |
| `404` | `AdministratorNotFound`, `RoleNotFound`, `SessionFamilyNotFound`. |
| `409` | `UserNameAlreadyExists`, `RoleNameAlreadyExists`, `RevisionConflict`, `IdempotencyConflict`. |
| `422` | `SelfDeactivationForbidden`, `SelfRoleMutationForbidden`, `SelfPasswordResetForbidden`, `LastActiveBuiltInAdministratorRequired`, `BuiltInRoleImmutable`, `RoleInactive`, `UnknownPermission`. |
| `500` | `InvariantViolation`; unexpected faults retain the existing sanitized NovaCore failure path. |

The management exception filter maps only the frozen `AdminAccessFailureKind` values, emits the
existing envelope and optional field details, and logs structured code/correlation context without
request bodies or secrets. Unique-key and EF concurrency failures are translated to the frozen
codes rather than leaking provider messages.

Existing `/api/auth/login`, `/api/auth/refresh`, `/api/auth/logout`, `/api/auth/codes`, and
`/api/user/info` routes, DTO shapes, cookie flags and path, refresh rotation/reuse behavior, and
15-minute access-token lifetime remain compatible. `codes` returns only effective permissions from
active roles. `user/info` retains all assigned role names for compatibility while its permission
codes remain effective-only. The M3.5 OpenAPI capture is additive, includes every permission,
header, envelope, enum, status, and DTO above, and must never emit password response, token, token
hash, EF entity, or persistence-receipt schemas.

## M35-02 Frozen MySQL Contract

This section freezes `M35-02-T03`. M3.5 extends the existing `NovaGameDbContext` and
`Operations/AdminAccess` mappings through one additive `AddAdminAccessManagement` migration. It
does not add another DbContext, database, physical module, distributed transaction, cache, token
store, or session-family aggregate.

### Schema Delta And Backfill

All GUID columns remain `char(36)` with ASCII collation and all timestamps remain UTC
`datetime(6)`. Existing primary keys, foreign keys, unique normalized-name constraints, membership
uniqueness, permission uniqueness, and token-hash uniqueness remain in force.

| Table | Frozen change |
| --- | --- |
| `admin_administrators` | Add `Revision bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 1`, map it as an EF concurrency token, backfill every existing row to `1`, and add `ck_admin_administrators_revision` requiring `Revision > 0`. Change `NormalizedUserName` to `utf8mb4_bin`. Add `ix_admin_administrators_active_name_id (IsActive, NormalizedUserName, Id)`. The existing unique normalized-name index supplies unfiltered prefix/order access. |
| `admin_roles` | Add `IsActive tinyint(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 1`, nullable `UpdatedAtUtc datetime(6)`, and `Revision bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 1`; backfill existing roles to active revision `1`; map revision as an EF concurrency token. Change `Name` and `NormalizedName` to `utf8mb4_bin`. Add stored generated `BuiltInRoleSlot tinyint AS (CASE WHEN IsBuiltIn = 1 THEN 1 ELSE NULL END)`, unique `ux_admin_roles_built_in_slot`, `ck_admin_roles_revision` requiring `Revision > 0`, and `ck_admin_roles_built_in_identity` requiring every built-in row to be active with exact binary names `Administrators` and `ADMINISTRATORS`. Add `ix_admin_roles_list (IsBuiltIn DESC, NormalizedName, Id)` and `ix_admin_roles_active_list (IsActive, IsBuiltIn DESC, NormalizedName, Id)`. |
| `admin_role_memberships` | Replace the single-column role index with `ix_admin_role_memberships_role_admin (RoleId, AdministratorId)`; retain unique `(AdministratorId, RoleId)`. Membership locks order by `AdministratorId`, `RoleId`, then `Id`. |
| `admin_role_permissions` | Change `Name` to `utf8mb4_bin`; retain unique `(RoleId, Name)` and `ValueGeneratedNever` identifiers. Permission locks order by `Name`, then `Id`. |
| `admin_refresh_token_sessions` | Retain token-hash and administrator-expiry indexes. Replace the family/revoked index with `ix_admin_refresh_sessions_family_order (FamilyId, CreatedAtUtc, Id)` and add `ix_admin_refresh_sessions_admin_family_order (AdministratorId, FamilyId, CreatedAtUtc, Id)` plus `ix_admin_refresh_sessions_family_replacement (FamilyId, ReplacedBySessionId)`. Add `ck_admin_refresh_sessions_revocation` requiring revocation timestamp and reason to be both null or both present, `ReplacedBySessionId` only for `Rotated`, and a replacement for every `Rotated` row. Existing coherent rows require no data rewrite. |
| `admin_audit_records` | Keep physical `AdministratorId` as the subject column and map it to the management DTO's `SubjectAdministratorId`. Add nullable `SessionFamilyId char(36)` and nullable `TargetRevision bigint`; add `ck_admin_audit_records_target_revision` requiring null or `> 0`. Change `Action`, `PermissionName`, and `CorrelationId` to binary comparison. Replace the two current audit indexes with the index set below. Historical rows retain null new fields and remain queryable. |

Add `admin_access_command_receipts` with no public API or navigation exposure:

| Column | Mapping |
| --- | --- |
| `Id` | `char(36)` primary key, caller-generated and never store-generated. |
| `ActorAdministratorId` | non-null `char(36)` logical actor reference with no foreign key. The actor was authenticated before claim, administrators cannot be deleted, and omitting the FK prevents receipt insertion from taking an implicit administrator lock ahead of the global order. |
| `CommandKind` | non-null `varchar(48)` with binary comparison; only `AdministratorCreate`, `AdminRoleCreate`, or `AdministratorPasswordReset`. |
| `IdempotencyKey` | non-null `char(36)` with ASCII binary comparison. |
| `RequestFingerprint` | non-null `binary(32)`. |
| `TargetResourceId` | non-null `char(36)`; deliberately no polymorphic foreign key. |
| `ResultingRevision` | non-null `bigint` with `ck_admin_access_receipts_revision` requiring `> 0`. |
| `CreatedAtUtc` | non-null UTC `datetime(6)`. |

`ux_admin_access_receipts_actor_kind_key` uniquely covers
`(ActorAdministratorId, CommandKind, IdempotencyKey)`, and
`ck_admin_access_receipts_command_kind` restricts the three exact values. Receipts are retained for
the lifetime of M3.5 authoritative data; no cleanup job may make an accepted key executable again.
They contain no password-derived value, response body, token material, request metadata, or audit
payload.

### Query Indexes And Bounded Projections

The audit index set is:

- `ix_admin_audit_records_time_id (OccurredAtUtc DESC, Id DESC)`;
- `ix_admin_audit_records_actor_time_id (ActorAdministratorId, OccurredAtUtc DESC, Id DESC)`;
- `ix_admin_audit_records_subject_time_id (AdministratorId, OccurredAtUtc DESC, Id DESC)`;
- `ix_admin_audit_records_role_time_id (RoleId, OccurredAtUtc DESC, Id DESC)`;
- `ix_admin_audit_records_action_time_id (Action, OccurredAtUtc DESC, Id DESC)`;
- `ix_admin_audit_records_success_time_id (Succeeded, OccurredAtUtc DESC, Id DESC)`;
- `ix_admin_audit_records_correlation_time_id (CorrelationId, OccurredAtUtc DESC, Id DESC)`.

Repositories project only the T02 DTO fields with `AsNoTracking` for reads. Optional equality
filters select the corresponding equality/time index; the mandatory half-open UTC range always
applies before paging. Correlation equality is case-sensitive. Administrator and role prefix
searches normalize with the same FormKC/invariant-uppercase rules as their value objects and use
parameterized prefix comparison; wildcard characters are data rather than SQL pattern syntax.

Administrator, role, session-family, and audit queries execute count and page reads inside one
short MySQL `REPEATABLE READ` root unit of work so `Items` and `TotalCount` share one consistent
snapshot. The `AdminManagement` verification scope asserts that isolation level. Paging and
filtering occur in SQL, page size never exceeds `100`, collections are never fully materialized,
and the server exposes no arbitrary sort or predicate language.

Session-family reads use one captured `nowUtc` and a database grouping over session rows:

- owner must be one distinct `AdministratorId`; multiple owners fail closed as
  `InvariantViolation`;
- `FirstCreatedAtUtc` is the minimum creation time; the terminal row is the sole family row whose
  own `ReplacedBySessionId` is null, independent of equal timestamps or random GUID ordering;
- `LastActivityAtUtc` is the maximum creation or revocation time; a revocation event wins an exact
  timestamp tie, and `LastIpAddress` comes from that event;
- any unrevoked row with `ExpiresAtUtc > nowUtc` makes the family `Active`;
- otherwise a terminal `Expired` revocation or an unrevoked expired terminal makes it `Expired`;
  any other terminal non-`Rotated` revocation makes it `Revoked`;
- normal rotated predecessors never make an otherwise active family appear revoked.

Zero or multiple terminal rows, zero or multiple unreferenced roots, disconnected replacement
chains, replacement links outside the family, replacement cycles, multiple unrevoked rows, or
multiple owners fail closed as `InvariantViolation`. These checks apply again under family locks
before rotation or revocation.

Authorization snapshots continue returning every assigned role name for `/api/user/info`, but
effective permission names join only active roles and exact registered permission names. Removed
catalog names and permissions from inactive roles never authorize a request.

### Global Lock Order

Every write path uses one total order. A path skips resources it does not need but never reverses
these categories:

1. atomically claim and lock one idempotency receipt key when the command requires it;
2. lock the unique built-in `Administrators` role row when last-active or seed policy is involved;
3. lock all other affected role rows by ordinal GUID, then their permission rows by name and ID;
4. lock affected administrator rows by ordinal GUID;
5. lock membership rows by administrator ID, role ID, and membership ID;
6. lock refresh-token rows by family ID, creation time, and session ID;
7. append audit rows and finalize the already claimed receipt immediately before the single commit.

`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` queries contain the matching `ORDER BY`; non-locking probes are always
re-read and validated after the ordered locks. The built-in role row is the serialization guard for
administrator active-state and role-replacement commands. Those commands lock it before counting
active administrators assigned to that role, so two concurrent commands cannot each remove one of
the last two administrators. Missing or malformed guard state fails `InvariantViolation`.

The authentication paths adopt the same order:

- login locks the normalized administrator row before password/active checks, optional password
  rehash, last-login recording, and new-family insertion, preventing a successful login from
  creating a family across deactivation or password reset;
- refresh and logout first perform a non-locking token-hash probe only to discover owner and family,
  then lock the administrator, then re-read and lock the complete family in canonical order, and
  finally revalidate exact token hash, owner, family, revocation, expiry, and account state;
- management family revocation probes the owner without exposing it, locks that administrator,
  locks the complete family, and revalidates one owner before mutation;
- administrator deactivation and password reset lock the target administrator before all of that
  administrator's families in family order.

This replaces the current token-first refresh/logout locking. No code path may hold a session lock
and then wait for an administrator or role lock.

### Command Transactions And Receipt Claim

Every command opens one transactional NovaCore unit of work over the single `NovaGameDbContext`.
State deltas, membership or permission children, forced session revocations, audits, and a receipt
commit together. Any exception, cancellation, audit failure, constraint failure, deadlock, or lock
timeout rolls the whole unit back.

Receipt commands issue one parameterized atomic
`INSERT ... ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE Id = Id` with a new candidate receipt ID, then select the unique
key `FOR UPDATE`. A stored ID equal to the candidate owns a new claim; a different stored ID is an
existing receipt. Existing fingerprints and target IDs are compared in constant-time where byte
data is involved. Matching receipts replay; mismatches return `IdempotencyConflict`. The candidate
contains the already known target ID and resulting revision (`1` for creates and
`ExpectedRevision + 1` for password reset). A later command failure rolls its new claim back.
Concurrent same-key requests block on the unique row; after commit one mutates and every matching
waiter replays, while after rollback one waiter becomes the new owner.

Command-specific locking is:

- administrator create: receipt, requested active roles by ID, administrator and membership
  inserts, aggregate and membership audit, receipt completion;
- role create: receipt, role and permission inserts, aggregate and permission audit, receipt
  completion; normalized-name uniqueness serializes competing names;
- display-name change: target administrator, canonical no-op/revision transition, aggregate audit;
- role replacement: built-in guard, probed union of current and requested roles, target
  administrator, target memberships, revalidation, delta and audit;
- active-state change: built-in guard, target administrator and memberships, last-active check,
  then target families for deactivation, state and audit;
- password reset: receipt, target administrator, all target families, credential/session/audit
  mutation, receipt completion;
- custom-role rename, active-state, or permission replacement: target role, then permission rows
  for replacement, state/delta audit;
- exact family revocation: probed owner administrator, complete family, changed-family audit.

Canonical no-ops write no state or audit. Receipt replays write nothing after reading current
redacted target state. Known normalized-user-name, normalized-role-name, receipt-key, and EF
concurrency constraint names translate to the T02 codes. Provider messages are never returned.
There is no provider-level automatic retry: deadlock, lock timeout, unknown unique failure, and
unexpected MySQL errors roll back and use the sanitized internal-failure path. A caller retries an
uncertain receipt command with the same key, a target-state command with the same intent, or family
revocation by exact family ID. Login and refresh are never automatically replayed.

### Migration And Seed Contract

The migration upgrades the existing M0.5 schema in place, preserves all rows, and changes no
GameConfig table. Defaults backfill administrators and roles before concurrency mappings and check
constraints become active. Adding the built-in singleton index rejects more than one built-in role;
the built-in identity check rejects a malformed marked row. A database with existing
administrators but no exact built-in role may migrate, but the immediately following seed step
fails closed before serving traffic. The explicit `Down` reverses new indexes, constraints,
collations, columns, generated slot, and receipt table to the exact pre-M3.5 model; it necessarily
discards M3.5 revisions, receipt evidence, role active state, and new audit metadata and is not an
approved production rollback after management writes exist.

DbMigrator is the sole seed writer during deployment. The seed contributor reads the entire
runtime `IPermissionDefinitionManager` catalog in ordinal group/name order instead of a fixed
array, locks the built-in role, validates its singleton identity and active state, and delta-syncs
its stored grants to the exact registered name set. One changed synchronization advances role
revision once and writes one audit per permission delta with the same target revision. A second run
changes no rows, revision, or audit. Missing or malformed built-in state with existing
administrators fails `InvariantViolation`; first bootstrap without administrators may create the
exact role and configured administrator in one transaction. Concurrent migrators are unsupported;
the deployment starts one DbMigrator, while named unique/check constraints make an accidental
second writer fail and roll back without partial seed state.

M3.5 neither changes `server/novacore.lock.json` (`222f5c59188d06098b677a7ff004c546871391ea`)
nor the separate external NovaCore checkout (clean at audit commit
`ef8db55173ff8f0286bb06abe753861c8a7d2d80`). These are distinct retained facts, not a requirement
that the commits match.

## M35-02 Frozen Vben And Browser Contract

This section freezes `M35-02-T04`. The generic admin remains the existing Vben Admin 5 `web-ele`
application using Vue 3, Element Plus, and the pinned workspace toolchain. It is an operational
client of T02 and never owns administrator, role, permission, session, audit, identity, time,
revision, concurrency, or idempotency policy.

### Static Navigation And Permission Composition

Add one static `Administrator Access` route group. Its parent appears only when at least one child
passes an explicit `Access AND leaf` predicate and redirects to the first authorized child. Do not
use a Vben authority-array form whose semantics are OR for this AND requirement.

| Path | Route name | Required effective permissions |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `/admin-access/administrators` | `AdminAccessAdministrators` | `Operations.AdminAccess.Access` and `Operations.AdminAccess.Administrators.Read`. |
| `/admin-access/roles` | `AdminAccessRoles` | `Operations.AdminAccess.Access` and `Operations.AdminAccess.Roles.Read`. |
| `/admin-access/sessions` | `AdminAccessSessions` | `Operations.AdminAccess.Access` and `Operations.AdminAccess.Sessions.Read`. |
| `/admin-access/audit` | `AdminAccessAudit` | `Operations.AdminAccess.Access` and `Operations.AdminAccess.AuditLogs.Read`. |

`Access` preserves the existing authenticated shell but reveals no management child by itself.
Action visibility uses the same explicit AND predicate:

- administrator create, display-name change, role replacement, and active-state change require
  `Access + Administrators.Manage`;
- password reset requires `Access + Administrators.ResetPassword` and is not implied by Manage;
- role create, rename, active-state change, and permission replacement require
  `Access + Roles.Manage`;
- family revocation requires `Access + Sessions.Revoke`;
- selectors additionally require their corresponding read route (`Roles.Read` for roles and the
  permission catalog); without it, the control is absent rather than populated from stale data;
- audit is read-only and has no mutation control.

Static filtering improves navigation only. Direct URLs, hidden controls, and client checks never
replace server authorization. This group contains no SwordMaster, player, equipment, reward,
economy, gameplay, or other game-owned route.

### Administrator Workspace

Use a compact server-paged table with user-name-prefix search, active-state, and role filters. It
shows immutable user name, display name, every assigned role with inactive roles visibly marked,
account state, last successful login, updated UTC time, revision, and a stable command column.

Creation uses a drawer with user name, display name, initial password, and active role selection.
Editing never exposes a user-name command. Display name and role changes use focused drawers;
activation, deactivation, and password reset use explicit confirmation dialogs. Password controls
use `autocomplete="new-password"` and are cleared on submit completion, failure abandonment,
dialog close, logout, and component unmount. Passwords are never echoed, cached, put in a URL,
logged, or retained in global state.

The UI may use the authenticated administrator ID to hide self-deactivation, self-role, and
self-password-reset controls. It does not reproduce last-active-built-in, active-role, or built-in
policy, and it renders the stable server rejection when stale UI state reaches the server.

### Role And Permission Workspace

Use a compact server-paged role table with name-prefix, active-state, and built-in-state filters.
It shows name, built-in and active state, effective registered permission count, assigned
administrator count, updated UTC time, revision, and commands.

A creation drawer accepts the custom-role name and initial permission set. Existing roles use
separate rename and permission-replacement drawers plus a focused active-state confirmation; no
interaction submits two role commands. Each drawer captures the ETag of its loaded detail, and a
successful command replaces the local detail and ETag before another command can begin. This
prevents ambiguous sequencing, stale follow-up revisions, and partial success inside one form.

The permission selector calls the runtime catalog endpoint, groups by server-provided group
display/name, searches locally within that bounded catalog, and submits exact names. It never
invents hierarchy, auto-adds `Access`, retains a removed catalog permission, or treats an inactive
role as effective. The built-in `Administrators` row is visibly read-only; rename, state, and
permission controls do not render for it. After any successful role active-state or permission
replacement, reload `/api/auth/codes` and `/api/user/info` before retaining management routes or
actions. If the signed-in operator lost required access, close the affected drawer/dialog, clear
its non-secret draft, and treat `403` from either refresh endpoint as an empty permission set:
clear local authenticated state and return to the existing login route because no authenticated
shell route is valid without `Access`. Otherwise redirect to the first still-authorized static
route. The server remains authoritative if permissions change concurrently.

### Session Workspace

Use a server-paged table with exact administrator and `Active | Expired | Revoked` filters. Consume
only `FamilyId`, `AdministratorId`, `UserName`, `DisplayName`, `FirstCreatedAtUtc`,
`LastActivityAtUtc`, `ExpiresAtUtc`, `LastIpAddress`, `State`, `RevokedAtUtc`, and
`RevocationReason`. Token values, hashes, row IDs, and replacement links never enter the generated
contract, store, DOM, log, screenshot, or clipboard.

Family revocation uses the exact family ID, no ETag or idempotency key, and an explicit
confirmation. Pending controls are disabled. Network retry retains the same family intent; a
successful repeated revoke simply renders the authoritative revoked state with no synthetic audit.
Reload authoritative list/detail state after each completed command.

`AdministratorId` is an exact GUID text input when the operator lacks
`Access + Administrators.Read`; the page must not call the administrator-list endpoint in that
state. With those additional permissions, replace it with a remote administrator lookup selector
backed by the authorized administrator-list endpoint; selection still submits only the exact GUID.
It never builds an incomplete selector from the currently loaded session page.

### Audit Workspace

The audit table is read-only, newest-first, and server-paged. On page initialization, capture one
client `nowUtc`; the default is `ToUtc = nowUtc` and `FromUtc = nowUtc - 7 days`. Every query sends
both values using the exact `[FromUtc, ToUtc)` maximum 31-day contract. Filters are exact action,
success, actor, subject administrator, role, and case-sensitive correlation ID. Actor and subject
are exact GUID text inputs when the operator lacks `Access + Administrators.Read`; role is an exact
GUID text input when the operator lacks `Access + Roles.Read`. With the respective additional
permission, replace those inputs with remote lookup selectors backed only by the authorized
administrator-list or role-list endpoint; selection still submits only the exact GUID. It never
queries an unauthorized endpoint or derives incomplete choices from the current audit page.
Changing a filter or time range returns to page one.

The table and detail drawer consume only the redacted T02 audit DTO. There is no edit, delete,
arbitrary query, raw payload viewer, or export. Invalid ranges get immediate local feedback and
remain server-validated. Long identifiers truncate visually inside bounded columns with a labelled
copy icon; they never widen the page or overlap adjacent commands.

### Typed Transport, ETags, And Idempotency

Create one management transport boundary from a checked real-API OpenAPI artifact. The tracked
capture is `admin/apps/web-ele/contracts/admin-access.openapi.json`; it contains only the 17 frozen
`/api/admin-access` operations and transitively required schemas. Pin `@hey-api/openapi-ts` exactly
at `0.99.0`, configure `admin/apps/web-ele/openapi-ts.config.mjs` with only the
`@hey-api/typescript` plugin, and own generated types under
`admin/apps/web-ele/src/generated/admin-access/`. A thin handwritten adapter under
`admin/apps/web-ele/src/api/admin-access/` maps the existing request client to those types and
preserves status, headers, `ApiResponse` error details, and cancellation.

`scripts/update-admin-access-contract.ps1` captures the management document from a composed real
API, validates its exact path/schema allowlist, generates into a temporary directory, normalizes
UTF-8/LF output, and atomically updates the tracked capture and generated output only outside
`-Check` mode. The exact drift command is
`pwsh -NoProfile -File scripts/update-admin-access-contract.ps1 -Check`; it regenerates in a
temporary directory, compares bytes with tracked artifacts, removes its temporary process/files,
and fails on any difference or empty output. Password response, token, token-hash, aggregate, EF
entity, and receipt schemas fail the allowlist guard. Existing auth and GameConfig handwritten
contracts remain compatible until separately migrated.

Create/detail/mutation responses retain their strong `ETag`. Every administrator display-name,
role, active-state, or password command and every role name, active-state, or permission command
sends the exact latest `If-Match`. Family revocation sends none. The UI never creates, increments,
or predicts a revision.

On revision conflict, preserve non-secret entered values, do not automatically retry, show the
stable conflict, and offer reload-and-review. A reload replaces the ETag and authoritative fields;
the operator must submit a new deliberate command. Secret input is cleared and must be re-entered.

Administrator and role creation generate one GUID idempotency key at the first submit. An unchanged
retry after an uncertain transport outcome reuses it; changing a non-secret authoritative value or
starting a new create intent generates a new key. Password reset generates one key when the
confirmed reset begins and reuses it only for uncertain transport retry. Because password values
are intentionally absent from the server
fingerprint, a reset retry with the same key always replays the first accepted secret; entering a
different password requires closing/restarting the reset intent and a new key. The same rule applies
to changing initial-password intent during administrator creation. An idempotency conflict is
shown and never silently replayed. A structured `4xx` rejection proves the command was not accepted
and retires the key; after correction, reload, or a new confirmation, the next submit uses a new
key. A successful accepted response closes the intent. Lack of a response and every `5xx` outcome
are uncertain because commit may have preceded response failure, so the exact same intent retains
its key and may only be deliberately retried unchanged.

Every list, filter, page, detail, and catalog request uses cancellation or monotonically increasing
request identity. A slow response may not replace a newer filter result, ETag, selection, detail,
or entity state.

### Operational States And Accessibility

Every workspace has stable-size loading, empty, validation, unauthorized, forbidden, not-found,
conflict, network-error, retry, pending-command, and success states:

- `401` uses the existing single refresh attempt and clears authenticated state after an
  unrecoverable refresh failure;
- `403` removes newly unauthorized controls/routes after permissions reload and shows a restrained
  forbidden result without treating visibility as authorization;
- a stale detail `404` closes that detail, retains non-secret unsaved input only for explicit
  review, and reloads the list;
- revision, duplicate-name, idempotency, and invariant failures retain non-secret operator input
  and display the stable server code/message;
- server field details map to their form controls when possible and otherwise appear at form level;
- no failed mutation is optimistically rendered as successful.

Security-sensitive commands require confirmation. Submit controls remain disabled while pending,
focus enters and returns from drawers/dialogs predictably, Escape never discards a pending command,
labels and error associations are accessible, icon-only commands have tooltips and accessible
names, and all filters, pagination, selectors, dialogs, drawers, and row commands work by keyboard.

### Visual And Responsive Contract

The visual thesis is a quiet, dense operations console: neutral work surfaces, existing Vben
typography and chrome, and one restrained action accent. The content plan starts directly with each
working table and its filters; there is no landing page, dashboard summary, hero, marketing copy,
feature tour, card mosaic, nested card, decorative gradient, or ornamental imagery. Motion is
limited to the existing fast drawer/dialog transitions and clear row/control state feedback; it
never delays repeated operations.

At `1440x900`, filters, primary command, table, fixed command column, and pagination fit the
working surface without body-level horizontal overflow. Tables may scroll within their own
container. Drawers use a bounded desktop width. At `390x844`, filters wrap, drawers become
full-width, pagination remains reachable, primary commands remain visible, and secondary row
commands move into a Lucide-icon menu with tooltips. Long names, permission lists, validation
messages, conflicts, dialogs, and confirmations never overlap, clip the actionable label, or make
a control unreachable.

### Focused And Real Browser Evidence

Vitest covers exact `Access AND leaf` route/action filtering, separate password-reset permission,
form limits, assigned inactive-role rendering, built-in-role read-only behavior, confirmation,
all ETag commands, create/reset key lifetimes, first-secret-wins retry, stale-response suppression,
secret clearing/redaction, paging, bounded audit dates, all operational states, keyboard behavior,
and mobile command layout.

The real Playwright suite contains exactly four M3.5 management tests, runs with zero retry/skip,
and retains the existing real GameConfig test as a fifth regression test:

1. `administrator lifecycle and concurrency`: login, create, display-name and role change,
   activate/deactivate, password reset, first-secret-wins retry, two-context stale ETag conflict,
   session refresh rejection after reset/deactivation, and logout;
2. `roles, permissions, and authorization`: custom-role create/rename/state/permission replacement,
   inactive-role effect, built-in read-only state, current-actor permission refresh, distinct
   `Administrators.Read`, `Administrators.Manage`, `Administrators.ResetPassword`, `Roles.Read`,
   `Roles.Manage`, `Sessions.Read`, `Sessions.Revoke`, `AuditLogs.Read`, and `Access` UI predicates,
   plus an authoritative `403` call for every isolated leaf;
3. `sessions and audit`: redacted family list, exact and repeated revoke, bounded audit filters,
   subject/actor/family/revision evidence, and retained-artifact secret scan;
4. `responsive and failure states`: the four workspaces at `1440x900` and `390x844`, long values,
   empty/validation/401/403/404/409/network-retry states, keyboard paths, and screenshots with no
   overlap, clipped command, body overflow, or inaccessible control.

Playwright uses the real Vben app, composed `NovaGame.Api`, DbMigrator, and disposable MySQL 8.4.
Setup may seed only the bootstrap administrator. Management state is created through the frozen
HTTP commands, never direct MySQL mutation. Vben mock responses, synthesized permission state,
intercepted business responses, direct repository substitution, or screenshots without the real
API are not acceptance evidence. The sole interception exception is an evidence-helper-owned,
one-shot transport abort bound to exact `GET
/api/admin-access/administrators?Page=1&PageSize=20` and one Playwright `Request` object for the
frozen network-retry scenario. It never fulfills, rewrites, replays, or synthesizes a business
response; every unmatched, duplicate, out-of-scope, or unconsumed failure fails the run.

The browser suite must not retain request bodies, cookies, authorization headers, traces, HAR, or
videos containing credentials or token material. Transport may carry required passwords and
secure refresh cookies to the real API, but the evidence harness records only redacted status,
route, correlation, and assertion metadata. Before every acceptance screenshot, assert that the
post-submit DOM, text and form-control values contain none of the submitted passwords, access or
refresh tokens, hashes, or connection strings. On every run, scan response bodies and every
retainable structured text report for those exact values and secret patterns. Raw API, DbMigrator,
and Playwright stdout/stderr are diagnostic transport only: they are deleted without becoming
retained evidence and are never copied into a thrown error. Binary screenshots exist only as
successful visual evidence inside the run root. Failure screenshots and every trace, HAR, video,
archive, or other binary diagnostic are removed before failed evidence may be retained. A DOM or
retained-text match, or a remaining binary failed artifact, fails acceptance.

The automatic Playwright evidence fixture observes the default `BrowserContext`; a test may create
an additional context only through the evidence-owned `newObservedContext`, which registers it
before returning it to the caller. It maintains an
in-memory exact-secret registry for submitted passwords, issued access tokens, rotating
refresh-cookie values, configured infrastructure secrets, and their relevant SHA-256
representations. Dynamic tokens cross to the parent verifier only through an authenticated,
acknowledged Windows named pipe and are never persisted or printed. A successful `POST` login or
refresh response has one narrow structural exception: exactly `data.accessToken` may carry one
valid JWT. The structured JSON visitor rejects duplicate keys, registers that token, replaces only
its exact raw source span with `[REDACTED]`, and scans both the remaining original text and parsed
structure. No other route, method, status, field, or duplicate token occurrence receives that
exception. The secure refresh cookie is read only from the exact auth URL, registered and
acknowledged before policy validation, checked for scope, flags, expiry, 32-byte Base64URL shape,
and rotation, and is never serialized. Before listeners are detached, the fixture closes every
registered browser context and proves the observed and closed context ledgers are equal; an
unregistered or late-request context fails the run.

Each exact browser test writes one redacted response-audit JSON report containing only source,
title, test status, exact browser/direct-request and context-ledger counters, the single frozen
expected network-failure method and path/query, response method, route without query, status, byte
count, scan completion, screenshot-surface scan count, and constant rule identifiers. Direct
`APIRequestContext` traffic must use the evidence-owned audited wrapper with exact first argument
`page.request`; acceptance specs may not issue a bare direct request or pass, alias, destructure,
return, import, cast, or bind another request source. Browser `/api` traffic is bound to the valid
root-only, credential-free HTTP(S) `NOVAGAME_E2E_BASE_URL` origin, direct traffic is bound to the
equally constrained `NOVAGAME_E2E_API_URL` origin, and the two origins must be distinct. Bare
`fetch`, Node HTTP/HTTPS/net/tls, Axios, Undici, child processes, dynamic module loading, and local
JavaScript transport helpers are prohibited. Repository verification uses the TypeScript compiler
to inspect every non-declaration source loaded by the E2E program beneath the admin root except the
exact evidence-owned helper; aliases, destructuring, property extraction, binding, and imported
local helpers cannot own direct requests, context creation, or request interception. Repository
verification also requires an exact no-extra-field report schema and manifest, raw duplicate-key
rejection, case-exact property names, real JSON arrays, a
positive response count equal to the ledgers, native JSON types, strict UTF-8 body scanning,
balanced request and context ledgers, every response marked scanned, and zero violations. It never
persists a request body, response body, cookie, authorization header, secret
value, or secret hash. `acceptance-evidence.ts` remains normalized-SHA-256 protected until M3.5
closure; `game-config.spec.ts` remains protected until `M35-07-T01` starts and consumes the frozen
browser design.

## M35-02 Frozen Verification Contract

This section freezes `M35-02-T05`. Evidence qualifies only when it is produced by the named
repository command, executes a positive test count, preserves the real dependency boundaries, and
is recorded in this goal after the command passes. A later stage may add concrete test locations
and result counts, but it may not remove, merge, rename, or weaken an evidence row.

### Evidence Rules

- Focused .NET evidence is emitted as TRX and must report `total > 0`, `executed > 0`,
  `passed = executed`, `failed = 0`, and `notExecuted = 0`. A missing result, counter, or
  zero-match filter fails even when `dotnet test` exits successfully.
- Focused Vitest evidence is emitted as JSON and must report `numTotalTests > 0`,
  `numPassedTests = numTotalTests`, `numFailedTests = 0`, and `numPendingTests = 0`. Missing JSON,
  missing counters, a zero-match pattern, skipped/pending tests, or a renamed test path fails.
- Playwright emits machine-readable JSON. The final suite contains exactly five tests: four exact
  M3.5 management tests and the retained one-test GameConfig regression. All five pass with zero
  failed, skipped, pending, flaky, unexpected, or retried outcomes. A missing result or counter
  fails.
- The automatic evidence fixture uses an in-memory exact-secret registry and sends dynamic access
  and refresh values only through an authenticated Windows named pipe. Its audited wrapper owns
  every direct API request, and its JSON visitor rejects duplicate keys. It redacts only the exact
  `data.accessToken` raw source span; every response otherwise receives strict UTF-8 body scanning.
  It closes every registered browser context before detaching listeners. Each test must produce its
  exact redacted response-audit JSON with balanced browser, direct-request, and context ledgers, a
  positive fully scanned response count, and zero violations. The normalized SHA-256 guard protects
  `acceptance-evidence.ts` until M3.5 closure and `game-config.spec.ts` until `M35-07-T01` starts.
- Failure screenshots and every trace, HAR, video, archive, or other binary diagnostic are
  non-retainable. Raw API, DbMigrator, and Playwright stdout/stderr transcripts are also always
  non-retainable. Playwright failure diagnostics may read the machine result only to match an exact
  registered test and return its fixed rule ID plus a validated source line and column; they never
  return child output, error text, stack, snippet, response content, or an unregistered title or
  path. Failed runs delete non-retainable files before the run-root text secret scan. Any
  remaining binary failed artifact fails verification.
- Before each screenshot, the evidence fixture scans the full DOM for every registered exact
  secret and high-confidence structured secret pattern, and scans rendered page text plus form
  values for those checks and the ambiguous 43-character Base64URL heuristic. Response bodies and
  retained text artifacts keep the full heuristic scan. Framework-generated structural markup,
  script text, and style text are therefore not treated as opaque tokens solely because one
  unrelated segment has that shape. Specs may invoke only the evidence-owned atomic
  `captureScreenshot(page, requiredPath)` operation: it validates a unique allowlisted path, scans
  immediately before the Playwright capture, and increments the scan/path ledgers only after the
  capture succeeds. A separate public scan or direct page, locator, element, or assertion
  screenshot is prohibited, and final scan/capture cardinality must match exactly.
- Successful screenshots are retained only through an exact relative-path allowlist. Before any
  deletion, verification requires a canonical signature, one first `IHDR`, at least one `IDAT`,
  one terminal `IEND` with no trailing bytes, and only the strict approved chunk allowlist. It then
  decodes each allowlisted PNG, checks its expected width and bounded full-page height, inspects the
  full decoded pixel buffer for opacity and diversity, records its SHA-256 and dimensions in
  process, and rejects missing, extra, corrupt, transparent, solid, or near-blank images even when
  adverse pixels align with a prior sampling stride. Every PNG byte sequence is scanned for each
  exact sensitive value and its retained hash representations before validated PNGs may be skipped
  by the retained-text scan. Any later operation, cleanup, secret, or legacy-output failure reruns
  binary removal with an empty allowlist before failed evidence may remain.
- Repository verification resolves the external NovaCore checkout from process `NOVACORE_ROOT`,
  defaulting to `D:\Programs\nova-core`, and requires a clean Git worktree at exact audit commit
  `ef8db55173ff8f0286bb06abe753861c8a7d2d80`. This is separate from the unchanged
  `server/novacore.lock.json` package snapshot commit
  `222f5c59188d06098b677a7ff004c546871391ea`; the two commits are intentionally not equal and are
  never compared to each other.
- `AdminManagement` is the cumulative focused server scope. It runs every frozen test family whose
  activation task is `in_progress`, `blocked`, or `complete`, and records an independent
  positive-count TRX for each. Families from later pending tasks are absent, not silently skipped.
  It uses the existing `NovaGame.EntityFrameworkCore.Tests` project, disposable MySQL 8.4 where
  required, and the retained Authentication regression. `AdminE2E` is the real five-test browser
  scope. Neither scope may fall back to a broad or full-suite success when one required selector
  matches zero tests.
- Tests may use deterministic clocks, fault injection at owned ports, and isolated database
  fixtures. Focused persistence tests may directly arrange baseline schema, malformed rows,
  invariant-violating graphs, or concurrent-writer fixtures solely to prove migration, constraint,
  fail-closed, rollback, and query behavior. Such arrangement is never public-workflow evidence.
  Composed API and browser acceptance may not use a Vben mock backend, response interception,
  synthesized permission state, repository replacement, a second DbContext, or direct MySQL
  mutation to create management state. Only the configured bootstrap administrator may be seeded;
  all later acceptance state uses the frozen HTTP commands. The pre-M3.5 GameConfig regression may
  retain its existing disposable SQL permission fixture only during M35-02 through M35-06 baseline
  verification; it is not M3.5 evidence. Before `AdminE2E` becomes executable at M35-07, replace
  that fixture with a custom role and administrator created through the frozen management HTTP
  commands, and remove its direct permission-table writes.
